<P align="left">
<B>Item&nbsp;9.&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<I>Regulation&nbsp;FD
Disclosure.</I></B>

<P align="left">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
On April&nbsp;14, 2003, Federal National Mortgage Association
(Fannie Mae) issued a press release relating to the
Company&#146;s earnings for the first quarter of fiscal year
2003. A copy of the press release is attached as
Exhibit&nbsp;99.1. On April&nbsp;14, 2003, Fannie Mae released a
monthly summary of financial data for March 2003. A copy of the
summary is attached as Exhibit&nbsp;99.2.

<P align="left">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
This information set forth under &#147;Item&nbsp;9. Regulation
FD Disclosure&#148; is intended to be furnished under
&#147;Item&nbsp;12. Results of Operations and Financial
Condition.&#148; We are furnishing this information under
Item&nbsp;9 in accordance with SEC Release No.&nbsp;33-8216.
Such information, including the exhibits attached hereto, shall
not be deemed &#147;filed&#148; for purposes of Section&nbsp;18
of the Securities Exchange Act of 1934, nor shall it be deemed
incorporated by reference in any disclosure document of the
Company, except as shall be expressly set forth by specific
reference in such document.

<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Fannie Mae Reports First Quarter 2003 Financial Results</B>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Net income at $1,941&nbsp;million, up 60.5&nbsp;percent over the first quarter of 2002;
Diluted earnings per share at $1.93, up 65.0&nbsp;percent</B>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Core business earnings at $1,850&nbsp;million, up 21.8&nbsp;percent over the first
quarter of 2002; Core business diluted earnings per share at $1.84, up 24.3
percent</B>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">WASHINGTON, DC &#150; Fannie Mae (FNM/NYSE), the nation&#146;s largest source of
financing for home mortgages, today reported financial results for the first
quarter of 2003.

</TABLE>
</CENTER>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">*&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Diluted
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">The company&#146;s reported results are based on generally accepted accounting
principles (GAAP). Management also tracks and analyzes Fannie Mae&#146;s financial
results based on a supplemental non-GAAP measure called &#147;core business
earnings&#148; (previously referred to as &#147;operating net income&#148; or &#147;operating
earnings&#148;). While core business earnings is not a substitute for GAAP net
income, management relies on core business earnings in operating Fannie Mae&#146;s
business because it believes that core business earnings provides management
and investors with a better measure of the company&#146;s financial results and
better reflects its risk management strategies than GAAP net income. Core
business earnings was developed in conjunction with the company&#146;s January&nbsp;1,
2001 adoption of Financial Accounting Standard No.&nbsp;133, <I>Accounting for
Derivative Instruments and Hedging Activities (FAS 133)</I>, to adjust for
accounting differences between alternative transactions used to hedge interest
rate risk that produce similar economic results but require different
accounting treatment under FAS 133.
</FONT>
<P align="center"><FONT size="2">(more)
</FONT>
<P align="center"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT>
<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Fannie Mae First Quarter Earnings<BR>
Page Two</B>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">The difference in the values and percentage changes between net income and core
business earnings, and EPS and core business EPS are entirely attributable to
those accounting differences for interest rate hedges. Refer to the attachments
to this release for a reconciliation of the company&#146;s non-GAAP financial
measures to its GAAP results.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Reported results</B>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Fannie Mae&#146;s reported net income for the first quarter of 2003 was $1,941
million, a 60.5&nbsp;percent increase compared with $1,209&nbsp;million in the first
quarter of 2002. Diluted earnings per share (EPS)&nbsp;were $1.93 in the first
quarter of 2003, up 65.0&nbsp;percent from $1.17 in the first quarter of 2002.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Strong growth in net interest income contributed significantly to the company&#146;s
reported results. Net interest income for the first quarter of 2003 was $3,368
million, up 38.6&nbsp;percent from the first quarter of 2002. This increase was
driven by an 11.7&nbsp;percent rise in the average net investment balance and a 29
basis point increase in the net interest yield.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">The company&#146;s net interest yield averaged 160 basis points in the first quarter
of 2003 compared with 131 basis points in the first quarter of 2002. Fannie
Mae&#146;s net interest yield benefited from an increase in the amount of purchased
options used as a substitute for callable debt, since the cost of these options
is not included in our net interest income or net interest yield. Prior to the
adoption of FAS 133 the company included the amortization of purchased option
premiums as a component of interest expense. This amortization expense is now
included as a component of purchased options expense on the income statement
and excluded from interest expense. Net interest margin, discussed below, is
calculated consistently with the company&#146;s previous methodology.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">In the first quarter of 2003 the company recorded $625&nbsp;million of
mark-to-market losses on purchased options compared with $787&nbsp;million in the
first quarter of 2002. These unrealized losses were recorded in accordance
with FAS 133<I>. </I>The reduction in the unrealized losses positively affected
reported results.
</FONT>
<P align="center"><FONT size="2">(more)
</FONT>
<P align="center"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT>
<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Fannie Mae First Quarter Earnings<BR>
Page Three</B>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Core business earnings</B>
</FONT>

<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Core business earnings for the first quarter of 2003 was $1,850&nbsp;million, a 21.8
percent increase compared with $1,519&nbsp;million in the first quarter of 2002.
Core business diluted EPS for the first quarter of 2003 was $1.84, or 24.3
percent above the first quarter of 2002. Growth in core business earnings and
diluted EPS was paced by a 10 basis point increase in the net interest margin,
a 34.1&nbsp;percent increase in guaranty fee income, and a $110&nbsp;million increase in
fee and other income.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Highlights</B>
<BR><BR>Highlights of Fannie Mae&#146;s financial performance in the first quarter of 2003
compared with the first quarter of 2002 include:
</FONT>
<P>

<P align="left"><FONT size="2">The annualized growth in the total book of business included a 34.0&nbsp;percent
increase in outstanding mortgage-backed securities (MBS)&nbsp;and 13.3&nbsp;percent
increase in the mortgage portfolio.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Franklin D. Raines, Fannie Mae&#146;s Chairman and Chief Executive Officer, said,
&#147;Fannie Mae recorded extremely strong and balanced growth during the first
quarter of 2003, continuing a record of financial performance that few other
companies have matched.&#148; Raines noted that compared with the first quarter of
2002 the company experienced double-digit growth in both its mortgage portfolio
and outstanding MBS, and also posted increases in its net interest margin and
average guaranty fee rate. Raines added that Fannie Mae&#146;s first quarter
credit-related losses were $14&nbsp;million below the previous quarter and $1
million below the first quarter of 2002. Said Raines, &#147;For the last few years
housing has been one of the notable bright spots of the U.S. economy. Fannie
Mae&#146;s disciplined response to the demands for credit from this sector has
benefited both home buyers and our shareholders.&#148;
</FONT>
<P align="center"><FONT size="2">(more)
</FONT>
<P align="center"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT>
<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Fannie Mae First Quarter Earnings<BR>
Page Four</B>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Fannie Mae&#146;s Chief Financial Officer, Timothy Howard, said that the company&#146;s
24.3&nbsp;percent growth in core business diluted earnings per share compared with
the first quarter of 2002 was paced by a 26.9&nbsp;percent
increase in core taxable-equivalent revenue over the same period. Strong
growth in core net interest income, MBS guaranty fees and fee and other income
all contributed to this increase, Howard said.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Howard said that during the three months ended March&nbsp;31, 2003 the company&#146;s
total book of business grew at an annual rate of 24.7&nbsp;percent. Howard said
that Fannie Mae&#146;s outstanding MBS grew at a 34.0&nbsp;percent annual rate during
this period, while the company&#146;s mortgage portfolio grew at a 13.3&nbsp;percent
annual rate. Howard noted that the company&#146;s mortgage portfolio had grown at a
slower rate than its outstanding MBS during seven out of the last eight
quarters. Said Howard, &#147;Fannie Mae takes a disciplined approach to portfolio
growth. Recently, commercial banks and other investors have been strong
bidders for fixed-rate mortgages. We have been able to accommodate these
demands by stepping up our issuance of MBS, and doing relatively less
purchasing of mortgages and MBS ourselves.&#148;
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Howard added that Fannie Mae reported improvements in all measures of capital
adequacy during the quarter, and at the same time took advantage of market
opportunities to repurchase 8.6&nbsp;million shares of common stock. Between
December&nbsp;31, 2002 and March&nbsp;31, 2003 the company&#146;s core capital rose from $28.1
billion to $29.5&nbsp;billion, while its excess of core capital relative to the
statutory minimum rose from $877&nbsp;million to $1.291&nbsp;billion. Fannie Mae&#146;s total
capital exceeded its risk-based capital requirement by $11.4&nbsp;billion at
December&nbsp;31, 2002 &#150; the latest date for which a risk-based capital requirement
has been determined &#150; compared with a $5.8&nbsp;billion surplus at September&nbsp;30,
2002. Fannie Mae&#146;s total capital plus subordinated debt was 3.7&nbsp;percent of
total assets at March&nbsp;31, 2003, approaching the 4.0&nbsp;percent target for this
measure set by the company in October 2000 as part of its voluntary
initiatives.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Outlook</B>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Howard said that in light of the company&#146;s higher-than-expected first quarter
earnings the company has changed its outlook for 2003 growth in core business
earnings from the guidance it gave in January. At that time Howard referred to
the then-current analyst consensus of 12 to 13&nbsp;percent growth in core business
diluted EPS for 2003 as &#147;a reasonable centering point for estimates.&#148; Said
Howard, &#147;We now expect to see a moderately higher growth rate in core business
earnings this year compared with our previous projection. We also expect the
pattern of change in our net interest margin to result in stronger quarterly
growth in the first half of the year than in the second half.&#148;
</FONT>
<P align="center"><FONT size="2">(more)
</FONT>
<P align="center"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT>
<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Fannie Mae First Quarter Earnings<BR>
Page Five</B>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Howard noted that continued declines in interest rates during the early months
of the year, and a resulting increase in projected mortgage liquidations, led
the company to maintain an unusually high percentage of short-term financing
for longer than it previously had envisioned. This higher percentage of
short-term debt,
coupled with its lower cost, caused the company&#146;s net interest margin to rise
further in the first quarter, rather than falling as had been anticipated.
Said Howard, &#147;Once interest rates level off or begin to move higher, we expect
to pay down significant amounts of short-term debt with the proceeds of
liquidating mortgages. As that occurs, our net interest margin should move
toward more normal levels.&#148; Howard said the company still describes such
levels as being &#147;in the area of 100 basis points or perhaps a bit lower,&#148; and
added that he expects the margin to return to that area by the end of this year
or early 2004.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Howard said that a reduced amount of losses on debt calls and repurchases in
2003 should partially offset the effects of a lower net interest margin during
the latter part of the year. Losses on debt calls and repurchases totaled $710
million in 2002.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Howard noted that while mortgage-to-debt spreads were relatively narrow during
the first quarter, the company has not changed its expectation for &#147;mid-teens&#148;
portfolio growth for the full year. Howard said, &#147;We do not expect the current
rate of growth in commercial bank holdings of fixed-rate mortgages to persist
indefinitely. Once the pace of bank buying cools, mortgage spreads should
widen and the volume of our portfolio purchases should pick up
correspondingly.&#148; Howard added, however, that if bank buying does not slow and
spreads remain narrow Fannie Mae would be content to grow its mortgage
portfolio more slowly.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Finally, Howard said that Fannie Mae continued to anticipate that its
credit-related losses will rise moderately in 2003, paced by a likely increase
in acquisitions of foreclosed properties. Howard noted, however, that the
company expected its credit loss ratio &#150; credit-related losses as a percent of
mortgages and MBS financed &#150; to remain under one basis point this year.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Fannie Mae is unable to provide an outlook for net income and net interest
yield, the most comparable GAAP measures to core business earnings and net
interest margin. These GAAP measures are heavily influenced by unrealized
gains or losses in the time value of purchased options, which depend on the
volume and mix of purchased options used to finance the company&#146;s portfolio as
well as fluctuations in interest rates which cannot reliably be projected.
</FONT>
<P align="center"><FONT size="2">(more)
</FONT>
<P align="center"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT>
<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Fannie Mae First Quarter Earnings<BR>
Page Six</B>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Details of Fannie Mae&#146;s first quarter 2003 financial performance follow.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Business volume</B>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Fannie Mae&#146;s business volume &#150; mortgages purchased for portfolio plus MBS
issues acquired by other investors &#150; totaled $335.9&nbsp;billion in the first
quarter of 2003, compared with $197.8&nbsp;billion in the first
quarter of 2002 and $304.5&nbsp;billion in the fourth quarter of 2002. Business
volume in the first quarter of 2003 consisted of $132.0&nbsp;billion in portfolio
purchases and $203.9&nbsp;billion in MBS issues acquired by investors other than
Fannie Mae&#146;s portfolio. This compares with $91.0&nbsp;billion and $106.8&nbsp;billion,
respectively, in the first quarter of 2002. Retained commitments to purchase
mortgages were $115.9&nbsp;billion in the first quarter of 2003 compared with $50.8
billion in the first quarter of 2002. Outstanding portfolio commitments for
mandatory delivery totaled $73.4&nbsp;billion at March&nbsp;31, 2003.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Fannie Mae&#146;s combined book of business &#150; the gross balance of mortgages held in
portfolio and outstanding MBS and other mortgage-related securities guaranteed
by Fannie Mae and held by other investors &#150; grew at a compound annual rate of
24.7&nbsp;percent during the first quarter of 2003, ending the period at $1.923
trillion. This growth resulted from a 13.3&nbsp;percent annualized growth rate in
the gross mortgage portfolio and a 34.0&nbsp;percent annualized rate of growth in
outstanding MBS.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Portfolio investment business results</B>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Fannie Mae&#146;s portfolio investment business manages the interest rate risk of
the company&#146;s mortgage portfolio and other investments. The results of this
business are largely reflected in core net interest income, which is net
interest income less the amortization expense of purchased options. Core net
interest income for the first quarter of 2003 was $2,604&nbsp;million, up 22.8
percent from $2,120&nbsp;million in the first quarter of 2002. This increase was
driven by a 11.7&nbsp;percent rise in the average net investment balance and a 10
basis point increase in the net interest margin.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Fannie Mae&#146;s net investment balance &#150; consisting of the company&#146;s liquid
investment portfolio together with its mortgage portfolio net of unrealized
gains or losses on available for sale securities, deferred balances, and the
allowance for loan losses &#150; averaged $872&nbsp;billion during the first quarter of
2003 compared with $781&nbsp;billion during the first quarter of 2002. The net
investment balance was $885&nbsp;billion at March&nbsp;31, 2003.
</FONT>
<P align="center"><FONT size="2">(more)
</FONT>
<P align="center"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT>
<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Fannie Mae First Quarter Earnings<BR>
Page Seven</B>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">The company&#146;s net interest margin averaged 125 basis points in the first
quarter of 2003 compared with 115 basis points in the first quarter of 2002 and
114 basis points in the fourth quarter of 2002. Fannie Mae&#146;s net interest
margin continued to benefit from an unusually steep yield curve and low
short-term interest rates, along with a benefit from the difference in timing
between the settlement of mortgage commitments, mortgage liquidations, and
funding.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">For the first quarter of 2003 the company realized losses from debt repurchases
and debt calls of $392.2&nbsp;million compared with losses of $171.7&nbsp;million in the
first quarter of 2002. During the quarter the company realized $377.8&nbsp;million
of losses on debt repurchases and $14.4&nbsp;million of losses on debt calls. Debt
repurchased and debt called in the first quarter totaled $3.6&nbsp;billion and $44.0
billion, respectively. Fannie Mae regularly calls or repurchases debt as part
of its interest rate risk management program.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Credit guaranty business results</B>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Fannie Mae&#146;s credit guaranty business manages the company&#146;s credit risk. The
results of this business are primarily reflected in guaranty fee income and
credit-related losses. Guaranty fee income was $546.6&nbsp;million in the first
quarter of 2003, a 34.1&nbsp;percent increase compared with the first quarter of
2002. The increase in guaranty fee income was driven by a 22.7&nbsp;percent rise in
average outstanding MBS and a 9.1&nbsp;percent increase in the effective guaranty
fee rate on that business. The effective guaranty fee rate in the first
quarter of 2003 was 20.3 basis points compared with 18.6 basis points in the
first quarter of 2002 and 20.4 basis points in the fourth quarter of 2002. The
increase in the guaranty fee between the first quarters of 2003 and 2002 was a
result of higher fee rates on new business, together with the faster
amortization of deferred fees due to accelerated prepayments.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Credit-related losses &#150; charge-offs plus foreclosed property income &#150; remained
very low in the first quarter, driven by a strong housing market and continued
home price gains. Credit-related losses totaled $20.4&nbsp;million in the first
quarter of 2003 compared with $21.5&nbsp;million in the first quarter of 2002.
Fannie Mae&#146;s credit loss rate &#150; credit-related losses as a percentage of the
average combined book of business &#150; was 0.4 basis points in the first quarter
of 2003 compared with 0.5 basis points in the first quarter of 2002.
</FONT>
<P align="center"><FONT size="2">(more)
</FONT>
<P align="center"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT>
<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Fannie Mae First Quarter Earnings<BR>
Page Eight</B>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Credit-related expense, which includes the provision for losses and foreclosed
property income and is the amount recorded on the company&#146;s income statement,
totaled $20.3&nbsp;million in the first quarter of 2003, in line with credit-related
losses. Fannie Mae&#146;s loss provision was $23.0&nbsp;million in the first quarter of
2003, compared with $28.3&nbsp;million in the first quarter of 2002. Foreclosed
property income was $2.7&nbsp;million in the first quarter of 2003 compared with
income of $6.6&nbsp;million in the first quarter of 2002, primarily due to gains on
foreclosed property dispositions. The company&#146;s allowance for loan losses and
guaranty liability for MBS totaled $808&nbsp;million at March&nbsp;31, 2003, unchanged
from December&nbsp;31, 2002.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Fannie Mae&#146;s conventional single-family serious delinquency rate, an
indicator of potential future loss activity, was 0.59&nbsp;percent at February&nbsp;28,
2003, compared with 0.57&nbsp;percent at December&nbsp;31, 2002. The
company&#146;s reporting of delinquent loans has been expanded from &#147;at risk&#148;
loans to include the performance of all seriously delinquent conventional
loans, whether or not they benefit from credit enhancement.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Fee and other income</B>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Fee and other income in the first quarter of 2003 totaled $113.3&nbsp;million
compared with $3.6&nbsp;million in the first quarter of 2002. The surge in first
quarter volume from a stronger refinancing market drove the combination of
transaction, technology, and multifamily fees to $240.8&nbsp;million, $154.1&nbsp;million
higher than the previous year. Partially offsetting the stronger fees were
higher operating losses from growth in tax-advantaged investments and lower
other miscellaneous income.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Fee and other income includes technology fees, transaction fees, multifamily
fees and other miscellaneous items, and is net of operating losses from certain
tax-advantaged investments &#150; primarily investments in affordable housing which
qualify for the low income housing tax credit. Tax credits associated with
housing tax credit investments are included in the provision for federal income
taxes.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Income taxes</B>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Provision for federal income taxes on net income was $706.9&nbsp;million in the
first quarter of 2003 compared with $362.3&nbsp;million in the first quarter of
2002. The effective federal income tax rate on net income was 27&nbsp;percent in
the first quarter of 2003 compared with 23&nbsp;percent for the same period last
year. The increase in the effective rate is attributable to an increase in
taxable income.
</FONT>
<P align="center"><FONT size="2">(more)
</FONT>
<P align="center"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT>
<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Fannie Mae First Quarter Earnings<BR>
Page Nine</B>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Provision for federal income taxes on core business earnings was $658.0&nbsp;million
in the first quarter of 2003, compared with $529.2&nbsp;million in the first quarter
of 2002. The effective federal income tax rate on core business earnings was
26&nbsp;percent in the first quarter of 2003, unchanged from the same period last
year.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Administrative expenses</B>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Administrative expenses totaled $343.8&nbsp;million in the first quarter of 2003, up
18.5&nbsp;percent from the first quarter of 2002. On January&nbsp;1, 2003, the company
adopted the fair value method for accounting for stock-based compensation and
began expensing all new stock-based compensation. The above-average growth in
expenses is related primarily to Fannie Mae&#146;s reengineering of its core
technology infrastructure to enhance its ability to process and manage the risk
on mortgage assets and the expensing of all new stock-based compensation.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">The growth rate of administrative expenses in 2003 is expected to decline
compared with 2002, but remain above historical levels as the company completes
its core infrastructure project.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">The company&#146;s ratio of administrative expense to the average combined book of
business in the first quarter of 2003 was .073&nbsp;percent, unchanged from the
first quarter of 2002. Fannie Mae&#146;s efficiency ratio &#150; administrative expense
divided by core taxable-equivalent revenue &#150; was 9.5&nbsp;percent in the first
quarter of 2003 compared with 10.2&nbsp;percent in the first quarter of 2002.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Capital</B>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Fannie Mae&#146;s core capital, which is the basis for the company&#146;s statutory
minimum capital requirement, was $29.5&nbsp;billion at March&nbsp;31, 2003 compared with
$28.1&nbsp;billion at December&nbsp;31, 2002 and $25.5&nbsp;billion at March&nbsp;31, 2002. Core
capital was an estimated $1,291&nbsp;million above the statutory minimum at March
31, 2003. At December&nbsp;31, 2002, core capital was $877&nbsp;million above the
statutory minimum.
</FONT>
<P align="center"><FONT size="2">(more)
</FONT>
<P align="center"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT>
<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Fannie Mae First Quarter Earnings<BR>
Page Ten</B>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Total capital includes core capital and the total allowance for loan losses and
guaranty liabilities for MBS, less any specific loss allowances, and is the
basis for the risk-based capital standard. Total capital was $30.3&nbsp;billion at
March&nbsp;31, 2003 compared with $28.9&nbsp;billion at December&nbsp;31, 2002 and $26.3
billion at March&nbsp;31, 2002. Fannie Mae&#146;s total capital exceeded the risk-based
requirement by $11.4&nbsp;billion as of December&nbsp;31, 2002, the latest period for
which a risk-based capital requirement has been determined. The risk-based
standard uses a stress test to determine the amount of total capital the
company needs to hold in order to protect against credit and interest rate
risk, and requires an additional 30&nbsp;percent capital for management and
operations risk. The higher of Fannie Mae&#146;s risk-based or minimum capital
standard is binding.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Fannie Mae repurchased 8.6&nbsp;million shares of common stock during the first
quarter of 2003, compared with 7.5&nbsp;million shares in the first quarter of 2002.
At March&nbsp;31, 2003 Fannie Mae had 981.0&nbsp;million shares of common stock
outstanding compared with 988.8&nbsp;million shares at December&nbsp;31, 2002.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">The company had $8.5&nbsp;billion of subordinated debt outstanding at March&nbsp;31,
2003. Subordinated debt serves as an important risk-bearing supplement to
Fannie Mae&#146;s equity capital, although it is not a component of core capital.
After providing for capital to support its off-balance sheet MBS, Fannie Mae&#146;s
total capital and outstanding subordinated debt as a percent of on-balance
sheet assets was 3.7&nbsp;percent at March&nbsp;31, 2003. The company issued $400
million of preferred stock during the first quarter of 2003. At March&nbsp;31,
2003, preferred stock made up 10.4&nbsp;percent of Fannie Mae&#146;s core capital.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Voluntary disclosures</B>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">As part of Fannie Mae&#146;s voluntary market discipline, liquidity, safety and
soundness initiatives of October 2000, the company discloses on a quarterly
basis its liquid assets as a percent of total assets along with the sensitivity
of its future credit losses to an immediate 5&nbsp;percent decline in home prices.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">At March&nbsp;31, 2003 Fannie Mae&#146;s ratio of liquid assets to total assets was 6.7
percent compared with 6.9&nbsp;percent at December&nbsp;31, 2002. Fannie Mae has
committed to maintain a portfolio of high-quality, liquid, non-mortgage
securities equal to at least 5&nbsp;percent of total assets.
</FONT>
<P align="center"><FONT size="2">(more)
</FONT>
<P align="center"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT>
<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Fannie Mae First Quarter Earnings<BR>
Page Eleven</B>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">At December&nbsp;31, 2002 the present value of Fannie Mae&#146;s net sensitivity of
future credit losses to an immediate 5&nbsp;percent decline in home prices was $596
million, taking into account the beneficial effect of third-party credit
enhancements. This compares with $501&nbsp;million at September 2002. The December
31 figure reflects a gross credit loss sensitivity of $1,838&nbsp;million before the
effect of credit enhancements, and is net of projected credit risk sharing
proceeds of $1,242&nbsp;million.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Fannie Mae&#146;s quarterly disclosures, together with the monthly
interest-rate-risk disclosures, are included in the attachments to this
release.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Derivatives and FAS 133</B>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Fannie Mae primarily uses derivative instruments as substitutes for noncallable
and callable debt issued in the cash markets to help match the cash flow
characteristics of its debt with those of its mortgages and reduce the interest
rate risk in its portfolio. Fannie Mae accounts for its derivatives under FAS
133.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">FAS 133 requires that Fannie Mae mark to market on its income statement the
changes in the time value, but not the total value, of its purchased options &#150;
interest rate swaptions and interest rate caps. The mark to market of the time
value of Fannie Mae&#146;s purchased options during the first quarter of 2003
resulted in a net mark-to-
market loss of $624.6&nbsp;million compared with a net mark-to-market loss of $787.2
million in the first quarter of 2002, which is reported on the purchased option
expense line of the income statement. Purchased option expense in the first
quarter of 2003 includes $764.3&nbsp;million in amortization expense, which was
included in net interest income prior to FAS 133 and currently is included in
core net interest income and in core business earnings. This amortization
expense represents the straight-line amortization of the up-front premium paid
to purchase the options over the expected life of the options.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">FAS 133 also requires that the company record any change in the fair values of
certain derivatives, primarily interest rate swaps it uses as substitutes for
noncallable debt, on the balance sheet in accumulated other comprehensive
income (AOCI), which is a separate component of stockholders&#146; equity. For
these types of transactions FAS 133 does not require or permit noncallable debt
to be marked to market. At March&nbsp;31, 2003, the AOCI component of stockholders&#146;
equity included a reduction of $15.8&nbsp;billion, or 1.9&nbsp;percent of the net
mortgage balance, from the marking to market of these derivatives. Accumulated
other comprehensive income is not a component of core capital.
</FONT>
<P align="center"><FONT size="2">(more)
</FONT>
<P align="center"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT>
<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Fannie Mae First Quarter Earnings<BR>
Page Twelve</B>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Fannie Mae&#146;s primary credit exposure on derivatives is that a counterparty
might default on payments due, which could result in Fannie Mae having to
replace the derivative with a different counterparty at a higher cost. Fannie
Mae&#146;s exposure on derivative contracts (taking into account master settlement
agreements that allow for netting of payments and excluding collateral
received) was $3.256&nbsp;billion at March&nbsp;31, 2003. All of this exposure was to
counterparties rated A-/A3 or higher. Fannie Mae held $3.0&nbsp;billion of
collateral through custodians to offset the risk of the exposure for these
instruments. Fannie Mae&#146;s exposure, net of collateral, was $256&nbsp;million at
March&nbsp;31, 2003 versus $197&nbsp;million at December&nbsp;31, 2002.
</FONT>

is 679906. Please dial in 5 to 10 minutes prior to the start of the call.
Fannie Mae will also provide an audio webcast of the conference call, which

Fannie Mae&#146;s Web site starting April&nbsp;14, 2003 at 7:00 p.m. ET. This press

<P align="center"><FONT size="2"><I># # #</I>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><I>Fannie Mae is a New York Stock Exchange company and the largest non-bank
financial services company in the world. It operates pursuant to a federal
charter and is the nation&#146;s largest source of financing for home mortgages.
Fannie Mae is working to shrink the nation&#146;s &#147;homeownership gaps&#148; through a $2
trillion &#147;American Dream Commitment&#148; to increase homeownership rates and serve
18&nbsp;million targeted American families by the end of the decade. Since 1968,
Fannie Mae has provided $4.5 trillion of mortgage financing for more than 49
million families. More information about Fannie Mae can be found on the
Internet at http://www.fanniemae.com.</I>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><I>Style Usage: Fannie Mae&#146;s Board of Directors has authorized the company to
operate as &#147;Fannie Mae,&#148; and the company&#146;s stock is now listed on the NYSE as
&#147;FNM.&#148; In order to facilitate clarity and avoid confusion, news organizations
are asked to refer to the company exclusively as &#147;Fannie Mae.&#148;</I>
</FONT>

<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>
<P align="center"><FONT size="2"><B>Glossary of Business Terms</B>
</FONT>

<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Amortization Cost of Purchased Options </B>&#150; the cost of purchased options
amortized over the original expected life of the options.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Business Volume </B>&#150; Mortgages purchased for portfolio plus MBS issues acquired by
other investors.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Combined Book of Business </B>&#150; The net mortgage portfolio plus outstanding MBS.
Also referred to as the book of business. (Formerly referred to as total book
of business).
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Core Capital </B>&#150; Total stockholders&#146; equity excluding other comprehensive income
(OCI).
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Core Net Interest Income </B>&#150; Net interest income and the amortization cost of
purchased options (Comparable to net interest income pre-FAS 133).
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Core Taxable Equivalent Revenue </B>&#150; The sum of net interest income, the
amortization cost of purchased options, guaranty fee income and fee and other
income, together with a taxable-equivalency adjustment for tax-exempt income
and investment credits (principally mortgage revenue bonds and low income
housing tax credit investments).
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Efficiency Ratio </B>&#150; Administrative expense divided by core taxable equivalent
revenue.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Gross Mortgage Portfolio </B>&#150; Unpaid principal balance of mortgages held in
portfolio, excluding deferred balances and the allowance for loss.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>MBS Issues Acquired by Other Investors </B>&#150; Lender-originated MBS issues less MBS
purchased by Fannie Mae&#146;s mortgage portfolio. Also referred to as MBS issues.
(Formerly referred to as net MBS issues). Does not include Fannie
Mae-originated MBS, which generally are immaterial and disclosed in a footnote.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Net Interest Yield </B>&#150; Taxable-equivalent net interest income divided by the
average net investment balance.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Net Investment Balance </B>&#150; The sum of Fannie Mae&#146;s net mortgage portfolio and
other liquid investments (including float).
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Net Mortgage Portfolio </B>&#150; Unpaid principal balance of mortgages held in
portfolio, less unamortized purchase premium or discount and deferred price
adjustments and allowance for loan losses.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Outstanding MBS &#150; </B>Mortgage-backed securities (MBS)&nbsp;and other mortgage
related-securities held by investors other than Fannie Mae&#146;s mortgage
portfolio. (Formerly referred to as net MBS outstanding).
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Realized Common Equity </B>&#150; Total stockholders&#146; equity excluding preferred stock
and OCI. Realized common equity is used in calculating return on equity.
</FONT>

<P align="center"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT>

regarding why management believes non-GAAP

measures, see &#147;Management&#146;s Discussion and Analysis of Financial Condition and Results of
Operations &#150; Core Business
Earnings and Business Segment Results&#148; in our Annual Report on Form&nbsp;10-K for the year ended
December&nbsp;31, 2002.</FONT></TD>

includes purchased options premiums amortized on a straight-line basis over the original
estimated life of the option. Presented net of tax.</FONT></TD>

        <TD><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>

partnerships and amortization expense of purchased options premiums, plus

<TR valign="top">
        <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">(1)</FONT></TD>
        <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
        <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">Represents unpaid principal balance on mortgages. Excludes the effect of
unrealized gains or losses on available for sale securities, deferred
balances, and the allowance for loan losses.</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR>
        <TD><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR valign="top">
        <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">(2)</FONT></TD>
        <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
        <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">MBS and other mortgage-related securities guaranteed by Fannie Mae.</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR>
        <TD><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR valign="top">
        <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">(3)</FONT></TD>
        <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
        <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">MBS and other mortgage-related securities guaranteed by Fannie Mae and
held by investors other than Fannie Mae&#146;s portfolio.</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR>
        <TD><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR valign="top">
        <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">(4)</FONT></TD>
        <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
        <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">Based on unpaid principal balances.</FONT></TD>
</TR>

        <TD><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>

        <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>

<TABLE width="100%" border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0">
<TR valign="top">
      <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">(1)</FONT></TD>
        <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
        <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">This amount represents the straight-line amortization of purchased options expense allocated to interest expense over the original expected life of the options. Included in core business earnings
instead of the unrealized gains and losses on purchased options to make it consistent with the accounting for the embedded options in our callable debt and the vast majority of our mortgages.</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR valign="top">
      <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">(2)</FONT></TD>
        <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
        <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">This amount represents unrealized gains and losses on purchased options recorded in accordance with FAS 133.</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR valign="top">
      <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">(3)</FONT></TD>
        <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
        <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">The reconciling item represents the net federal income tax effect of core business earnings adjustments based on the applicable federal income tax rate of 35&nbsp;percent.</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR valign="top">
      <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">(4)</FONT></TD>
        <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
        <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">Reflects non-GAAP adjustments to permit comparison of yields on tax-exempt and taxable assets based on a 35&nbsp;percent marginal tax rate.</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR valign="top">
      <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">(5)</FONT></TD>
        <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
        <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">Includes the effect of purchased options amortization expense allocated to interest expense over the original expected life of the options.</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR valign="top">
      <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">(6)</FONT></TD>
        <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
        <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">Annualized taxable-equivalent net interest income divided by the weighted average net investment balance.</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR valign="top">
      <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">(7)</FONT></TD>
        <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
        <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">Annualized taxable-equivalent core net interest income divided by the weighted average net investment balance.</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR valign="top">
      <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">(8)</FONT></TD>
        <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
        <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">Represents non-GAAP adjustments for tax credits related to losses on certain affordable housing tax-advantaged equity investments and other investment tax credits using a 35% marginal tax rate.</FONT></TD>
</TR>
</TABLE>

<P>
<TABLE width="100%" border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0">
<TR valign="top">
    <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">&#149;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">Effective duration gap &#151; measures the extent the effective duration of the portfolio&#146;s assets
and liabilities are matched. A positive duration gap indicates that the effective duration
of our assets exceeds the effective duration of our liabilities by that amount, while a negative
duration gap indicates the opposite.</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR>
    <TD><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR valign="top">
    <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">Effective January 2003, the duration gap is a weighted average for the month. Prior to 2003,
the duration gap was calculated on the last day of the month.</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR>
    <TD><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR valign="top">
    <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">&#149;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">Net interest income at risk &#151; compares Fannie Mae&#146;s projected change in portfolio net interest
income under the financially more adverse of a 50 basis point increase and decrease in interest
rates. Fannie Mae also compares the expected change in portfolio net interest income for
the more adverse of a 25 basis point decrease and increase in the slope of the yield curve.
Both measurements are done for one-year and four-year periods.</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR>
    <TD><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR valign="top">
    <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">A positive number indicates the percent by which net interest income could be reduced by the
increased rate shock. A negative number would indicate the percent by which net interest
income could be increased by the shock.</FONT></TD>
</TR>
</TABLE>

    <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">Lifetime credit loss sensitivity measures the sensitivity of Fannie Mae&#146;s expected future credit losses to an immediate

and other credit enhancements.</FONT></TD>

<DOCUMENT>
<TYPE>EX-99.2
<SEQUENCE>4
<FILENAME>w85410exv99w2.htm
<DESCRIPTION>MONTHLY SUMMARY RELEASE
<TEXT>
<HTML>
<HEAD>
<TITLE>exv99w2</TITLE>
</HEAD>
<BODY bgcolor="#FFFFFF">
<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>


<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><IMG src="w85410fmbiglogo.gif" alt="Fannie Mae Logo 1">
</FONT>
<P align="center"><FONT size="2"><B>Statement by Jayne Shontell, Senior Vice President for Investor Relations<BR>
On March Financial Data</B>
</FONT>

<P align="left"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Our monthly results for March continue to show strong results from a
robust housing market and our disciplined growth strategies.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Highlights include very strong growth in outstanding MBS and our total
book of business. Following portfolio growth in January of 34.6&nbsp;percent and in
February of 9.5&nbsp;percent, purchase and commitment volumes declined in March as
tighter mortgage-to-debt spreads made such activity less attractive, and Fannie
Mae&#146;s portfolio contracted slightly as a result. As noted in our earnings
press release, however, we have not changed our expectation for mid-teens
portfolio growth for the full year.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Risk measures, including the portfolio duration gap, remained favorable.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;This monthly release includes three changes, which are highlighted below:
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Enhanced Credit Delinquency Disclosures</B>
</FONT>

<P align="left"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The March Monthly Summary includes Fannie Mae&#146;s enhanced delinquency
disclosures reported and included in our initial 10-K filing with the
Securities and Exchange Commission on March&nbsp;31.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The change in reporting of delinquent loans has been expanded from &#147;at
risk&#148; loans to include the performance of all seriously delinquent conventional
loans with and without third party credit enhancement. Included in the 10-K
and beginning with the March Monthly Summary, we will report the single-family
delinquency rate for three categories:
</FONT>
<P>
<TABLE width="100%" border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0">
<TR valign="top">
    <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">1.</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">Non-Credit Enhanced, which includes high quality loans without primary
mortgage insurance (PMI)&nbsp;or other credit enhancements. As of December&nbsp;31,
2002, these high-quality loans represented 73&nbsp;percent of our conventional
single-family book.</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR>
    <TD><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR valign="top">
    <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">2.</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">Credit Enhanced, which includes loans that have PMI or other credit
enhancements such as supplemental pool insurance or lender recourse.</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR>
    <TD><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR valign="top">
    <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">3.</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">Total, for all loans included in the previous two categories (where loan
level data is available).</FONT></TD>
</TR>
</TABLE>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;As is detailed in the 10-K, loans where Fannie Mae assumes full risk have
lower serious delinquency rates than loans where it shares the risk with
partners. This reflects Fannie Mae&#146;s disciplined strategy of pricing for risk
and using risk sharing on higher-risk loans.
</FONT>
<P align="center"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT>
<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Duration Gap Provided As A Monthly Average</B>
</FONT>

<P align="left"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Starting this month, we are releasing the duration gap as a monthly
average, and this will be the duration gap number we will release going
forward. Previously a duration gap for the last business day of the month was
published. This change will reduce the chance that a financial event on the
last day of the month will make this measure unrepresentative of the
portfolio&#146;s average risk position for the month as a whole.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In order to provide a complete data set for 2003 we are publishing today
the monthly average duration gap for January and February. As seen in the chart
below the difference between the month-end duration gap and the monthly average
duration gap for the first three months this year was approximately one month.
</FONT>
<CENTER>
<TABLE cellspacing="0" border="0" cellpadding="0" width="55%">
<TR valign="bottom">
    <TD width="5%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="61%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="5%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="5%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="1%">&nbsp;</TD>

</CENTER>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>Net Interest Margin And Net Interest Yield To Be Released Quarterly.</B>
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;To carry out our compliance with the SEC&#146;s new rule on the use of non-GAAP
financial measures, effective March&nbsp;28, 2003 for all public companies, Fannie
Mae will report net interest margin and its GAAP equivalent, &#147;net interest
yield,&#148; on a quarterly basis only.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Net interest margin is a non-GAAP measure. The GAAP equivalent is &#147;net
interest yield.&#148; A full reconciliation of these two measures requires both net
interest income and purchased options expense, two income statement items, and
therefore can only be done on a quarterly basis when we publish the quarterly
income statement. (See Fannie Mae&#146;s First Quarter 2003 Financial Results press
release issued today for the margin, net interest yield and the reconciliation
of the two measures for the first quarter, 2003.)
</FONT>
<P align="center"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT>
<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>

<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<P align="center"><FONT size="2"><B>MARCH 2003</B>
</FONT>

<P align="left"><FONT size="2">Fannie Mae&#146;s regular monthly financial disclosures for March reflect the
company&#146;s continued record of disciplined growth.
</FONT>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>HIGHLIGHTS FOR MARCH INCLUDE:</B>
</FONT>

<TABLE width="100%" border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0">
<TR valign="top">
    <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">&#149;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">Total business volume rose to $108.8&nbsp;billion, the third
largest on record.</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR>
    <TD><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR valign="top">
    <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">&#149;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">Fannie Mae&#146;s book of business grew at a compound annual
rate of 23.2&nbsp;percent.</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR>
    <TD><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR valign="top">
    <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">&#149;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">Outstanding MBS grew at a compound annual rate of 45.3
percent, the highest in a decade.</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR>
    <TD><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR valign="top">
    <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">&#149;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">Retained commitments were $39.5&nbsp;billion and portfolio
purchases were $34.3&nbsp;billion. The portfolio balance fell
at a 1.1&nbsp;percent annual rate while outstanding portfolio
commitments were a strong $73.4&nbsp;billion at month-end.</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR>
    <TD><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR valign="top">
    <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">&#149;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">Risk measures continued to be favorable. Fannie Mae&#146;s
portfolio duration gap moved to minus two months.</FONT></TD>
</TR>
</TABLE>
<P align="left"><FONT size="2"><B>DISCLOSURE CHANGES:</B>
</FONT>

<TABLE width="100%" border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0">
<TR valign="top">
    <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">&#149;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">The monthly summary table includes expanded delinquency
disclosures, providing more information on single-family
and multifamily along with a break-out of single-family
conventional loans with and without credit enhancement.</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR>
    <TD><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR valign="top">
    <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">&#149;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">Effective this month, the duration gap will be reported as
a weighted average for the month. Previously a duration
gap for the last business day of the month was published.
We have published new numbers for January and February
consistent with March so that all of 2003 is on the same
basis.</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR>
    <TD><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
</TR>
<TR valign="top">
    <TD width="1%" align="left" nowrap><FONT size="2">&#149;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="3%"><FONT size="2">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
    <TD width="96%"><FONT size="2">To carry out our compliance with the SEC&#146;s new rule on the
use of non-GAAP financial measures, effective March&nbsp;28,
2003 for all public companies, Fannie Mae will report net
interest margin and its GAAP equivalent, &#147;net interest
yield,&#148; on a quarterly basis only. Net interest margin is
a non-GAAP measure. The GAAP equivalent is &#147;net interest
yield.&#148; A full reconciliation of these two measures
requires both net interest income and purchased options
expense, two income statement items, and therefore can
only be done on a quarterly basis when we publish the
quarterly income statement.</FONT></TD>
</TR>
</TABLE>
